# Findings 0751 to 1000

249 findings: 226 confirmed, 14 unconfirmed, 9 refuted. [How the register works](README.md).

<a id="f751"></a>
### #751 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003e4750 — beNDMWLoadIntf2 pad face buttons

Pad face-button semantics, resolved against retail and consistent across vfunc_31 and the SelSlot helpers: 0x4000 (Cross) is confirm/advance and 0x2000 (Circle) is cancel/back. In CTRL st0==3 the 0x4000 bit gates the advance branch while 0x2000 fires SQ_CANCEL (0x516d50); in st0==2 the 0x4000 bit confirms the j_linewin01 window; in ss_update (0x3e1760) the 0x4000 bit runs the select (0x3ee190) and 0x2000 the cancel. The verifier's 'confirm'/'cancel' flow labels were therefore inverted; they are renamed to cross-confirm/circle-cancel while their pad sequences are unchanged.

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/loadintf2.c

**Where:** 0x003e4750 — scripts/verify_loadintf2.py oracle descent

The load-interface differential suite now descends into all ten recovered bodies on the retail side (the interpreter executes their ORIGINAL R5900 instructions) while the C side runs the recovered bodies: the seven SelSlot helpers 0x3e0b80/0x3e0bb0/0x3e0c40/0x3e1760/0x3e2a80/0x3e2b10/0x3e3070 and the text-order trio 0x3ebce0/0x3ebe00/0x3ec050. A real_hits set records every REAL jal target and the run asserts it covers REAL; the matrix is 432 message + 1029 update cases (40 random + 13 deliberate 8-tick flows + 1 regression) plus the interpreter self-test. The 13 flows pin slot entry, movement (SQ_MOVE), confirm/select (0x3ee190), cancel (SQ_CANCEL), backtracking, delayed completion and the ss_pub_cursor path.

**Notes:** Topic head for beNDMWLoadIntf2 vfunc_31 differential verification coverage: also read #744, #748 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Where:** 0x003e4280 — beNDMWLoadIntf2ComMdlCtrl completion accessors + common select

The slot screen's completion/selection decisions are recovered in recovered/frontend/completion.{c,h} (#753). Ownership from retail and reflection.json: 0x003e4280 is CommMdlCtrl(a0)._openDataList(+0x14).count(+8) <= 0 (an igObjectList: +8 count, +0x10 data), 0x003e44d0 the same on _closeDataList(+0x18) -- both 20-byte accessors called from vfunc_31 and the SelSlot helpers with the COMMDLCTRL record. 0x003ee190 is the selection: for i in 0..3 over common(+0).+0x28 -> list -> +0x10 data -> [i*4] it sets _closeKind(+0x24)=1, _st2/+0x28 _st3/+0x2c _isUseData/+0x30 = 0, the run byte(+0xf)=0 and the timer(+8)=0x3c, then clears the selected row (sel): _closeKind=0, +0xf=1, +8=0. ### #754 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003e4750 — scripts/verify_loadintf2.py completion wiring

Completion is no longer forced (#754): the verifier dropped the rv overrides for 0x3e4280/0x3e44d0 (which returned a canned 1) and wires the state the recovered accessors read -- CommMdlCtrl._openDataList/_closeDataList at CM+0x14/+0x18 with counts written per tick from the cmon/cmcn keys of set_pad -- plus all four per-player records at RECARR[0..3] (RECALT+i*0x40 for players != p) so ds_select has real targets. Fifteen deliberate 8-tick slot flows now assert the intended transitions: slot-entry opens on tick 3, slot-delayed waits until the open queue drains (tick 5), slot-stall never opens (queue never drains), slot-complete settles (SELSLOT+0x35) exactly when the queue empties, slot-cancel sets +0x36, and slot-confirm arms the per-player records via ds_select. Update matrix 1045 cases; the 0x3ee190 select is no longer a call event.

**Evidence:** scripts/verify_loadintf2.py

<a id="f755"></a>
### #755 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003ebe00 — text-order focused coverage

Text-order branch coverage audit (#755): the ten focused direct cases distinguish branches actually exercised from merely reaching each function. 0x3ebe00 (to_set): present id 0x100c walks its whole 2-line list (2x 0x38d860) and fires the on-token 0x517a28 (id&0xf000==0x1000); id 0x503 (6-line list, bit 20 set, id&0xf000==0) fires the off-token 0x517a18; a MISSING id (0x7ffff) hits the 0xffffffff sentinel and makes no calls and no writes. Correction to #749: the flag==1 bit-20 path does NOT publish the next entry line list (the 0x38d860 count is unchanged); it latches the FOLLOWING entrys text pointer into 0x58a220 (verified equal to the table word at 0x4cda44+27*0xc) and repeats the animation dispatch (0x389ff0 twice vs once). 0x3ec050 (to_pub, close variant) publishes the line list but does not latch 0x58a220 and fires no anim token. Boundary: 0x3ec050 has no sentinel, so a missing id would run off the id table (callers always pass present ids); not exercised.

**Evidence:** scripts/verify_loadintf2.py

### #756 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003e3b80 — Common-model completion / native title integration

The contiguous common-model helper group 0x003e3b80..0x003e46a4 (ten bodies, 2784 bytes) is behaviorally recovered in commdl.c plus the existing completion predicates. Reflection owner is beNDMWLoadIntf2ComMdlCtrl: messenger +0x10, open/close lists +0x14/+0x18. Entries are beLoadIntf2ComMdlData (_ramNo +8, _openSQ +0xc, _infoName +0x10). 0x3e3b80 generates/plays, 0x3e3e00 adds a copied vec3, 0x3e4030 plays/open-tracks, 0x3e42a0 plays/close-tracks. Their wait flag is low-byte-equals-one, not general nonzero. 0x3e4210 queries ISSEQNAME with four arguments total; 0x3e4480 queries ISRUN with three. Intervening 0x3e3d80 destroys a reference wrapper, not the controller.

**Evidence:** Retail instruction bodies cross-checked with mipsel-linux-gnu-objdump; data/reflection.json handles 0x58be38, 0x58be68, 0x58be70. scripts/verify_commdl.py executes all ten original bodies: 2676 comparisons, ordered engine calls and non-stack stores plus final bytes. Allocator/list/string/messenger leaves remain boundaries; no byte-matched source or rendering claim.

<a id="f757"></a>
### #757 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003e44f0 — Common-model completion / native title integration

Common-model completion polling visits open requests newest to oldest and stops at the first ISSEQNAME result whose low byte equals one. Close requests are independently visited newest first with ISRUN and the same stopping rule. Completed entries release their low-23-bit object reference, remove by index, then zero the new tail. Repeated requests are not merged. 0x3e44f0 drains/polls, not fills; the submission helpers append the requests. The port now uses this recovered queue layer instead of per-model tracked/removing flags.

**Evidence:** 2676 retail/C helper checks include blocking by the newest request, independent close progress, duplicates, low-byte answers, draining/re-entry, and string/reference branches. Native title integration compares 2921 ticks against original title and queue instructions with the same bounded lower model executor; it does not verify that executor against console animation timing, pixels or audio. Slot harness queues remain seeded.

**Where:** 0x00394ae0 — Model controller JUMP/WCHECK behavioral recovery

The contiguous JUMP/WCHECK/no-op group (68+180+920+8 bytes) is behaviorally recovered in modelwait.c. JUMP calls owner vtable +0x8c with a null-name empty-string fallback and returns 1. WCHECK start interns command+8 into work+0x4c and returns 0. WCHECK update resumes Action on any nonzero ISCOMEND result; TurnY/Landing/Moving/Rotation resume when the motion query low byte is zero. <a id="f761"></a>
### #761 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00230110

FUN_00230110 is the engine's float-to-int64 conversion: a sign-magnitude wrapper around the soft-double conversion FUN_0022ffe8. Negative inputs (c.lt.s against zero) convert -x and negate (dnegu); the magnitude is truncated toward zero; magnitudes >= 2^64 saturate to 2^64-1 (so -x becomes 1); values in [2^63,2^64) wrap as unsigned; negative zero and denormals convert to 0. Every Action2 time conversion goes through it. Recovered as ee_f32_to_s64 / a2_f32_to_s64.

**Notes:** FUN_00230110 / FUN_0022ffe8 retail instructions (soft-double helpers 0x22e440..0x230f44 executed, not stubbed) compared against recovered C and independent Python integer arithmetic over every exponent 0..255 x both signs x six mantissa classes plus 1,500 random patterns (4,587 vectors): recovered/frontend/eefloat.c, scripts/verify_action2time.py. Mutating the 2^64 saturation is rejected. EE FPU rounding is a separate unconfirmed model (see the EE FPU finding).

**Evidence:** scripts/verify_action2time.py; docs/engine/action2-timing.md

<a id="f762"></a>
### #762 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00398e80

FUN_00398e80 (112 B) = node clock, a2_node_time(node, clock): ticks = low 32 bits of (clock >> 18) (arithmetic shift, then truncation); f = (float)(unsigned)ticks, converted as cvt.s.w for ticks < 2^31 and as halve-with-sticky-bit + cvt.s.w + add.s(f,f) otherwise; time = ((int64)(f * node._timeScale(+0x78)) << 18) + node._timeBias(+0x80), where (int64) is 0x00230110. The clock is an int64 nanosecond count and the >>18 / <<18 pair quantizes it to 262,144 ns steps. This corrects the wording 'clock frame count' used in earlier prose: a tick is 2^18 ns, not a 60 fps frame. The same sequence is inlined in JOINTSET Start/Update, the WAIT timer start/elapsed and the node rebasing in vfunc_33.

**Notes:** Retail instructions of 0x398e80 (with 0x230110 and the soft-double helpers executing) vs recovered C: 984 calls over 12 scales (zero, negative, denormal, max, non-trivial), 55 clocks (tick-boundary neighbours, 2^49/2^50 edges, negatives, int64 min/max, random) and seven biases; independently asserted with Python integer arithmetic. Extends #619. Topic head for Node clock FUN_00398e80 formula: also read #619 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/action2time.c; scripts/verify_action2time.py

<a id="f763"></a>
### #763 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003a7a20

The contiguous group 0x003a7a20 (736 B) / 0x003a7d00 (128) / 0x003a7d80 (272) / 0x003a7e90 (312) / 0x003a7fd0 (364) is the named timer registry kept on an action's property list (act+0x28, created lazily with a reference count whose release test uses only the low 23 bits). Timers are keyed by name: 'WAIT' (0x50dfd8, WAITCHECK Frame), 'FADE' (0x50dfe0, FADESET with duration 0), 'FLASH'/'ACTION' strings adjoin. 0x3a7a20 = start(act,name,duration int64): lookup, create+name+register when absent, then write base = -(f(clock)<<18) at +0x18, start clock at +0x28, 1.0f at +0x38, duration at +0x20. 0x3a7fd0 = elapsed = ((int64)(timer.scale(+0x10)*ticks)<<18)+base. 0x3a7e90 = complete: missing timer -> 1, otherwise duration(+0x20) < elapsed as STRICT signed 64-bit. 0x3a7d80 = exists. 0x3a7d00 = release *ref and delete ref when (int16)flags > 0. Names are hypotheses (a2_timer_*), not committed symbols.

**Notes:** Retail instructions of all five bodies vs recovered C over list/allocator/reference-count/null-allocation/reentrant-list matrices (gp flag 0/1, counts with high bits, leaf-installed lists, creation failure), timer scale and clock alignment matrices, and the threshold duration = E-1, E, E+1 and int64 extremes; strictness asserted independently (mutating < to <= is rejected). 3,580 timer cases; ordered leaf calls and stores identical. Leaves: property lookup/insert/name, allocation, destroy, delete.

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/action2time.c; scripts/verify_action2time.py; docs/engine/action2-timing.md

<a id="f764"></a>
### #764 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003764c0

beAction2WAITCHECK_Start/Update behavior recovered. work+0x44 states: 1 Frame (timer 'WAIT'), 2 Motion, 3 Joint. Start (mgr, act, comp) dispatches on comp type string[0]: J->3, M->2, F->arm timer for (int64)(1e9f*secs/60.0f) then state 1; 'R' with name[3]=='S' stores secs in f32 global 0x58a348 and returns 1; other 'R' computes secs'=global+(float)(r)/100 with r a virtual query (mgr+0x10->+0x30, vtable +0x60) modulo trunc(100*secs-100*global) (unsigned divide; zero divisor leaves the dividend) and continues as Frame; unknown types do nothing; all but 'RxxS' return 0. Update (mgr, act): clears work+0x44 BEFORE calling StartComponents(0x377700) on completion; Joint completes when the low byte of work+0x30 is nonzero; Frame completes when the WAIT timer completes; Motion drives 0x1dd4b0/0x1af510 and completes when the motion position is not unsigned-below the end time (zero end completes at once; a controlling element whose work+8 != 1 suppresses the check). The purpose of the R mode (beat-aligned wait) is an unconfirmed hypothesis; its arithmetic is verified.

**Notes:** Retail instructions of 0x376340 and 0x3764c0 vs recovered C: 1,946 Start cases (14 type strings, 18 float operands incl. negative/huge/denormal, rhythm global and query variants, null type) and 344 Update cases (all states, jointIsEnd byte/word values, motion lists with end-time threshold +/-1); the armed duration for 30 frames asserted as 499,999,968 ns independently. Mutating the clear-before-restart store order is rejected.

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/action2time.c; scripts/verify_action2time.py; docs/engine/action2-timing.md

<a id="f765"></a>
### #765 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00376de0

beAction2JOINTSET_Update/Start behavior verified against retail, resolving the strictness question: the update changes anything only while end < now (strict, signed 64-bit); at now == end nothing happens. State 2 clamps to end; state 1 subtracts (end - start) once per iteration (loop wraps); a change rebases node._timeBias to the new time and sets _jointIsEnd. Start writes state (1 iff loop string[0]=='L'), converted endpoints (+0x20/+0x28), rebases the node bias to the range start and clears +0x30; returns 1. A loop whose start >= end spins forever in retail (reference identical, never tested). Resolves the open question in #618/#619 about threshold strictness.

**Notes:** Retail instructions of 0x376de0 and 0x376f40 vs recovered C: 1,800 Update cases (states 0-3 and -1, four scales, six clocks incl. the 2^49 sign-of-tick edge, three biases, end at now-2^20..now+2^20) with clamp and wrap results and repeat-tick stability asserted independently, and 897 Start cases (loop strings, ranges, scales, clocks). Mutating < to <= is rejected. Topic head for beAction2JOINTSET Start/Update behaviour: also read #618 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/action2time.c; scripts/verify_action2time.py; docs/engine/action2-jointset.md; docs/engine/action2-timing.md

<a id="f766"></a>
### #766 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00377700

The completion chain around the component walker: a2_start_components (0x377700) resumes at work._comIndex and starts each component by class (MOTIONSET, JOINTSET, FADESET, WAITCHECK, FLASHSET tested in that order, one virtual GetMetaObject call per test), continuing while the handler's low return byte is nonzero and storing the index back; an unknown class spins in retail. The per-frame driver 0x3778d0 (vfunc_31) runs only while act+0x14 is set and _isFreeze (work+0x4c byte) is clear, calling MOTIONSET, JOINTSET, FADESET, WAITCHECK, FLASHSET updates in that order, each gated on its work slot - so JOINTSET updates before WAITCHECK in the same frame. ISCOMEND (vfunc_33 message case 0, 0x377a40) answers true iff work+0x44 == 0 and work._comIndex == component count. For the logo, completion is conditional on the modeled EE arithmetic and supplied clock. At scale 1.0, and while both absolute tick counts remain exactly representable below 2^24 with no low-word wrap, OPEN/CLOSE complete when the tick difference reaches 1908. Only in this range does the 30/31-frame phase rule at a 16,666,667 ns step apply. Outside this range each absolute clock must be rounded before subtraction: start clocks ((2^25)+1)<<18, (2^31)<<18, and ((2^31)+255)<<18 complete on frames 30, 33, and 29 respectively under that clock model. The first finishes after 1907 raw ticks. The system clock and EE hardware behavior remain unconfirmed (#767/#768).

**Notes:** Retail instructions of 0x377700, 0x3778d0 and the ISCOMEND path of 0x377990 vs recovered C: 86 component/driver/predicate cases plus 37,350 lifecycle steps (OPEN/CLOSE over 13 start phases x 5 clock cadences, jittered cadences, half-speed scale, and random interleavings of restart, re-entry, freeze, deactivate and state queries); the completion tick is asserted from independent integer arithmetic each frame. The other message cases of vfunc_33 and the system clock's source and cadence are unrecovered.

**Evidence:** recovered/frontend/action2time.c; scripts/verify_action2time.py; docs/engine/action2-timing.md

<a id="f772"></a>
### #772 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00330af0 — igPsx2LongTimer lifecycle

The contiguous igPsx2LongTimer runtime group 0x00330a50..0x00330e58 is behaviorally recovered. Read returns _additionalTime when stopped, otherwise _additionalTime+raw-_startTime. Resume clears _isStopped before sampling raw and rebases _startTime. Stop captures elapsed before setting the flag. Reset copies _resetTime and resamples only if running; stop/reset freezes at the preset; restart always clears stopped, copies preset and samples raw. Integer operations wrap at 64 bits.

<a id="f773"></a>
### #773 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00237730 — igInsightCore -> igSerialScheduler -> beAction2 order

igInsightCore writes _frameTime(+0x40), _currentTime(+0x38), then _worldTime(+0x50) before dispatcher(+0x20) and serial scheduler(+0x0c) callbacks. Increment is arithmetic_shift_right_18(low64(delta*sign_extended_int32(scale))), without a backwards-time clamp. Scheduler 0x00237ec0 snapshots manager refs and calls slot+0x74. The captured logo path enters 0x00378bc0 then Action2 slot+0x84=0x003778d0, observing the newly written world clock.

**Notes:** Both logo states place manager 0x00703e28 at scheduler index17; action 0x0137ef50 is group43 element0, with gates open. <a id="f789"></a>
### #789 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0038d0a0; 0x0038e8f0 — beMessenger::vfunc_27 / timed enqueue

Timed Lua SD callbacks can enqueue zero/positive-delay messages during the original expiry pass; its budget is the reusable record list size (32 initially), not pending count. Negative-delay insertion during a callback can remove the inserted child and leave an idle record blocking an already-due peer.

**Notes:** Original-instruction oracle: 9 zero/positive follow-up samples plus two 32-record chain passes match native. Negative-delay fixture emits only PARENT on retail versus PARENT/CHILD/PEER natively. Receiver callbacks, context and list/allocation services are controlled leaves. This establishes an instruction-level edge case, not an observed gameplay bug. Native detaches the executing message for lifetime safety; bug-compatibility policy/default for this difference is unresolved. Host publication-scope inheritance is a separate lifetime policy, not a retail ownership claim.

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_scriptservices_retail.py; DW4_Decomp/asm/Gap__Bec__beMessenger__vfunc_27.s (0x0038d100..0x0038d3d0); DW4_Decomp/asm/FUN_0038e8f0.s (0x0038ebb8..0x0038ec40). The AI consumer 391cf0 reaches it through virtual slot 140 (391fa8), so SQ_SILENT's permanent MUTEKI ON -1 ends when the NEAR map selects SQ_WAIT.

**Notes:** Only three retail writers of Work+124 exist in 0x38xxxx..0x3axxxx: MUTEKI command 395070, countdown 3951e0 (positive counts only) and this block. Case 6 (397b40) rejects any nonzero MUTEKI unless both names start with 'p'. Without the clear a woken far Digi-Elf stayed untalkable.

<a id="f930"></a>
### #930 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00428790 — beNDMWShopCtrl30 update / SUBWND BANK

BANK SERVICE (SUBWND mode 3): beNDMWShopCtrl30 update 428790..429628 is an item-and-money bank, not a currency exchange. Main 0 = ShopSelect01 menu 1350 (deposit/withdraw/leave, result stored at +84), main 1 = menu 1355 (items/money). Items (main 2/3) drive the shared StatusCtrlD0 list: deposit moves DEVICEFOLDER[i] to BANKDEVICE unless BANKDEVICE has 0x60 records (notice 0x54E) or the item is equipped; withdraw moves BANKDEVICE[i] to DEVICEFOLDER unless DEVICEFOLDER has 0x24 records (notice 0x555) and shows 0x55E when the bank empties; prompts 1357/1364; an empty bank on withdraw goes to main 6 (0x55E). Money (main 4/5) creates ShopCtrlEditBit with the menu choice; a result other than -1 adds/subtracts it between BIT and BANKBIT unvalidated, then SUBWND UPDATE. Native: port/src/bankshop.c, hub SUBWND mode 3.

**Notes:** Retail has no transfer validation: the editor's limit is the only bound. The native transactions validate and the window fails if a record changed so the transfer is invalid. Equipped test (item+0x10) is interpreted as the equipped flag.

**Evidence:** port/src/bankshop.c; port/src/inventorydetail.c; port/docs/bank-shop.md; port/tools/test_hubbank.c; port/tools/test_playerinit.c

<a id="f931"></a>
### #931 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004332D0 — beNDMWShopCtrlEditBit update / ShopSelect34 digit selector

BANK MONEY EDITOR: beNDMWShopCtrlEditBit update 4332d0 (mode = parent menu: 0 deposit, 1 withdraw, 2 nine-digit transfer) presents j_shwin SQ_BITPUTINOPEN/SQ_BITDRAWOUTOPEN, tracks STWINFONT36 labels and STWINFONT29/font100, runs txt_shop 1301/1375 and the prompt 1360/1367, then a ShopSelect34 (factory 3b45d0, 7 digits, variant = mode) selects the digit. Limit: deposit min(BIT, 9999999-BANKBIT), withdraw min(BANKBIT, 9999999-BIT). Up (horizontal bit 1 or digital 0x10) wins over down (4/0x40) at the cursor place when SELECTCTRL i_stcursor03 ISSELECTNOW; a changed place redraws, a changed value also plays CURSOR_MOVE. Amount in STWINFONT25 font000..006 (deposit) or font010..016 (withdraw) with the other balances in font01x/font00x/font02x (4402e0/440110, zero-padded width 7-selection). Cross with a positive amount publishes it to the parent's result and shows acknowledgement 0x552/0x559; otherwise -1.

**Notes:** The selector's move latch clears only on a later input frame, so the first cursor tap after a move is consumed (faithful). Mode 2 (txt_shop 1378, STWINFONT36 only) is not implemented natively. Colours and digit geometry are not compared pixel-for-pixel with retail.

**Evidence:** port/src/bankshop.c; port/src/statuscontent.c dw4_statuscontent_digits; port/src/statusbit.c dw4_statusbit_edit; port/docs/bank-shop.md; port/tools/test_hubbank.c

### #933 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00429630 — beNDMWShopCtrl40 update / CNOS service

CNOS SERVICE (SUBWND mode 4): beNDMWShopCtrl40 update 429630..42a2d8 is the item appraiser. State 0 prepares the STWINFONT29/font100 prompt box through 3f5410 (blank string, LEFT). Main 0 opens the shared StatusCtrlD0 list (factory 3baf10) with +0x6D=1 (unknown_enable: only the placeholder equipment weapon 276 / armor1 31 / armor2 44 is selectable) and +0x6F=0 (no auto close), runs txt_shop 1450 and waits for the list. A -1 result closes (main 3); a ready selection stores the DEVICEFOLDER index (+54), reads BIT and goes to main 2 (BIT < 300, notice 1451 via ShopSelect00 0x5AB then main 3) or main 1. Main 1: ShopSelect00 0x5AC (1452), then ShopSelect01 0x5AD (1453, two entries); entry 0 debits 300 BIT, sends SUBWND UPDATE and rerolls the item record, then either answer restores the selection and restarts the list. Main 3 closes the list and waits for it to disappear; state 2 clears queued text and requests the children to close.

**Notes:** Native owner: port/src/cnosshop.c. The 300 price is the literal 0x12C. Equipment projections follow the record (no RECALCPLAYER, only SUBWND UPDATE).

**Evidence:** port/src/cnosshop.c; port/include/dw4/cnosshop.h; port/tools/test_hubcnos.c; port/docs/cnos-shop.md; asm/Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrl40__vfunc_21.s

<a id="f934"></a>
### #934 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00429630 — beNDMWShopCtrl40 update / CNOS reroll

CNOS REROLL: the tier is chosen from the player's LEVEL with signed compares (<41 -> 1, <101 -> 2, <201 -> 3, <301 -> 4, <401 -> 5, otherwise 6). The item kind selects the DATABASE coweapon (0), coarmor1 (1) or coarmor2 (2) through DATABASE/GETDATABASE. Row tier*100 holds the entry count in its ID column; rows tier*100+1.. hold a result record ID, a RATE weight and a RANK (read and discarded). The draw is genrand (vtable slot 24 of the shared MT19937 object reached via *(obj+0x10)+0x30) modulo 10000u; the first entry whose running RATE sum is >= the draw sets the DEVICEFOLDER record, and a miss leaves it unchanged. The 300 BIT debit applies either way.

**Notes:** The three tables exist in the Central hub's database owner and are numeric-only (zero string rows). Native: dw4_cnos_tier/dw4_cnos_roll/dw4_playertransactions_cnos_convert; tools/test_playerinit.c pins thresholds, running-sum boundaries and atomicity with a synthetic table.

**Evidence:** port/src/playertransactions.c; port/tools/test_playerinit.c; port/tools/test_hubcnos.c; docs/systems/rng.md

### #935 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00429780 — beNDMWShopCtrl40 update / _retCode default

CNOS _retCode INITIAL VALUE (inference): the first main-0/sub-1 tick tests +30 == -1 before the list has published anything, so retail must start the field at a non-negative value (zero-initialised window memory); a -1 would close the service on its first update. BANK and XDATA never expose this because a menu writes +30 first. The native window starts it at 0. Also unverified: an empty DEVICEFOLDER (unreachable in retail because equipped gear cannot leave the inventory) would publish index 0 from the single blank list row; the native window ignores a selection that is not a weapon/armor item.

**Notes:** The field's default is not set in beNDMWShopCtrl40_InitFields (3b85c0), which only registers the field metadata; the base window constructor was not traced.

**Evidence:** port/src/cnosshop.c; port/docs/cnos-shop.md

A selection reads the held count from the DISKFOLDER entry whose record equals the row: nine held goes to notice 0x623, otherwise ShopSelect52 (factory 3b4410, 9-held rows at (52,-69,0), scripts 0x624/0x5DE) picks a quantity (labels STWINFONT32 font010 quantity, font021 quantity*HANBAI). A balance below quantity*HANBAI shows notice 0x622; otherwise ShopSelect00 0x613 then a yes/no ShopSelect01 0x614; yes subtracts quantity*HANBAI from BIT, sends SUBWND UPDATE and adds the quantity to the matching DISKFOLDER entry's +0x14. Retail adds nothing when no entry matches but still charges (unreachable: all twelve records exist).

**Notes:** Recovered from asm/Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrl50__vfunc_21.s with an m2c draft plus the decoded string tables (4cf8a0 font names, 4cf990 SQ_OPEN%02d cursor table); helpers 42b880 (quantity/total texts), 42b9d0 (hide STWINFONT32 font020/010/021) and 42bae0 (i_stcursor04 open/close). ### #937 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00434790 — beNDMWShopCtrlSell / DeviceSell / DiskSell

DISK SELL BRANCH: ShopCtrlSell (434790) reads DEVICEFOLDER (any record) and DISKFOLDER (any disk with +0x14 > 0). With neither it shows ShopSelect00 0x628 and closes (mother _retCode = -1); otherwise it opens ShopSelectB0 (factory 3b3f40, script 0x625) with a decide list {0,1} (no devices), {1,0} (no disks) or none, then child 3b70e0 (DeviceSell 4353e0) for entry 0 or 3b6e60 (DiskSell 436ac0) for entry 1, and recomputes after either ends. DeviceSell lists DEVICEFOLDER twelve per page with NAME and KAITORI, enabled iff unequipped (+0x10 == 0) and KAITORI > 0; a selection shows notice 0x619 and yes/no 0x61A; yes sets BIT = min(BIT + KAITORI, 9,999,999) (compare >= 0x989680, store 0x98967f), removes the item from the folder, sends SUBWND UPDATE, steps the cursor back (page back when on the first row) and hides the arrows on the first/last page; emptying the folder closes the window. DiskSell lists the twelve DISKFOLDER entries (NAME, held digit, KAITORI), enabled iff held > 0, opens ShopSelect52 at (52,-104,0) with held rows (scripts 0x624/0x5E1, labels STWINFONT30 font010/021/020) and credits quantity*KAITORI with the same cap.

**Notes:** Decide-list constants read from the retail image: 554a50..64 = 0,1,1,0,1,0. KAITORI is the item database column of the item's own kind (weapon/armor1/armor2/abChip) or conItem for disks. Native: dw4_playertransactions_disk_sell/sell_item and dw4_statuscontent_sell_device/sell_disk.

**Evidence:** port/src/diskshop.c; port/src/inventorydetail.c; port/src/statuscontent.c; port/tools/test_hubdisk.c; port/tools/test_playerinit.c; asm/Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrlSell__vfunc_21.s; asm/Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrlDeviceSell__vfunc_21.s; asm/Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrlDiskSell__vfunc_21.s

<a id="f940"></a>
### #940 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003f82a0 — beNDMWGameRam CURRENT2BASE/BASE2CURRENT

EVOLUTION RECORD COMMANDS: CURRENT2BASE (3f8760) copies LEVEL, EXP, SKILL[9] and UPCNT[11] into BASE_LEVEL[d], BASE_EXP[d], BASE_SKILL[d*9..] and BASE_UPCNT[d*11..] (d = DIGIMONNO). BASE2CURRENT (3f82a0, species chosen by 3f80e0) sets DIGIMONNO, DIGIMONNAME = shinka MODEL_NAME, and loads LEVEL/EXP/SKILL/UPCNT from that species; for player zero it also re-binds the common FLAG/FLAGFOLDER/COUNTER to BASE_FLAG*. 3f80e0 returns the first of shinka rows 0..15 whose MODEL_NAME equals the argument (else 0).

**Notes:** m2c drafts of 3f8760, 3f82a0 and 3f80e0 with constants decoded from rodata (518848 DIGIMONNO, 518858 LEVEL, 518880 EXP, 518b30..b60 BASE_*). Native: dw4_playertransactions_evolve (to_base/from_base).

<a id="f941"></a>
### #941 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003f8c90 — beNDMWGameRam weapon sockets

WEAPON COMMANDS: WEAPONEQUIPCHECK (3f93c0) is true when an owned weapon (DEVICEFOLDER kind 0) has (SOUBI & (1 << species)) != 0 and the skill at its SKILL index (SKILL for the current species, BASE_SKILL[species*9..] otherwise) is >= SKILLPOINT. WEAPONEQUIPSTRONG (3f8c90 with flag 1) clears every weapon equipped flag (+0x10), empties POWERSOCKET, picks the eligible weapon with the highest TSUYOSA (first on ties) as the only equipped weapon in socket 0 and leaves POWERSOCKETNAME with just its MODEL (none when nothing is eligible). With flag 0 it equips the first three weapons instead.

**Notes:** m2c drafts of 3f93c0 and 3f8c90 with decoded constants (518a40 DEVICEFOLDER, 518a88 POWERSOCKET, 518aa0 POWERSOCKETNAME, 518c10 SOUBI, 518c18 SKILLPOINT, 5188b0 TSUYOSA). ObjectList_FillRetained on the socket list is read as emptying; the native socket list keeps its three entries.

**Evidence:** port/src/playertransactions.c; port/tools/test_playerinit.c

GOODS LIST (ShopCtrlSales, services 6..9): stock comes from SUBWND GETSHOPDATA; page count is ceil((last present slot + 1)/12) (the list size when nothing is present); a page lists its leading present slots (1..12 rows, a sold-out chip row is disabled); the E1 selector at (-121,128,0) carries the decide bytes. **Notes:** Decoded from the m2c draft of 438160 and its helpers, the rodata strings (51b3xx..51bdxx) and the retail tables (4cf8a0 fonts, 4cfa70 SQ_OPEN%02d). Verified headless by port/tools/test_hubshops.c (WPU/APU/CHIP/JUNK purchases, refusals, page turn, sold-out slot).

### #950 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00448190 — FUN_00448190 / FUN_004480a0 / stock selection

STOCK ITEM ROLLS: weapons and blue armor roll a pocket count (448190: v = draw%620 + 1 - ((level/10+10)/10); 1 -> 5, <4 -> 4, <8 -> 3, <16 -> 2, <32 -> 1, else 0) and every weapon/armor an enhancement (4480a0: first = draw%6000 + 1, second = draw%level + 1; first == 1 gives level+1, first < 3001 gives second - first/10, else 0, clamped at 0), using the engine MT stream in item column order. Chips take no draws. Stock selection (3d8740): WPU row = species base 25/50/75/100/125/0 (150 with two or more joined players) + level tier, APU/CHIP row = level tier, JUNK row from the COUNTER[1] investment tiers; a stored -1 and an absent column are both the missing sentinel; JUNK reads a per-entry kind column NNkind.

**Notes:** m2c drafts, the recovered preparation reference and the tables decoded from the retail image. Verified by test_playerinit (rolls, rows) and test_hubshops (prepared stock of all four modes against the hub tables).

<a id="f953"></a>
### #953 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003fca10 — SYSctlDiskFolder / hub disk-collector service

CTRLITEM DISK (GAMERAM receiver, case 17 at 3fd368; kind string compared at 3fd970): the 12-entry DISKFOLDER entry whose record (+0xc) equals the id argument (448050, -1 if absent) holds its count at +0x14. Operation names SET0/CLR1/IS2/ISMAX3/ISEQUIP4/GET5/ADD6 (strings 5190e0..519128). SET adds amount, saturating at 9, and answers false when the count is already 9; CLR subtracts amount clamped at 0 and answers false when already 0; IS answers count != 0; ISMAX answers count == 9. ISEQUIP, GET and ADD push no answer and change nothing. Arguments are (player, id, 0, amount).

**Notes:** Executed from the retail ELF by tools/verify_playertransactions_retail.py against dw4_playertransactions_disk_folder: 400 randomized states plus the three no-answer operations match. Retail indexes the list with -1 for a missing id and has no guard for negative amounts; the native transaction refuses both (-1).

**Evidence:** port/docs/prmame-npc.md

<a id="f954"></a>
### #954 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003722c0 — engine services over the CTRLITEM receiver

Engine wrappers SYSctlDiskFolder(op, player, id, amount) at 3722c0 and SYSctlDeviceFolder(op, player, kind, record, revision, pocket) at 3721b0 both send GAMERAM CTRLITEM: the strings are "DISK"+op+"" with ints (player, id, 0, amount), or "DEVICE"+op+kind with ints (player, record, revision, pocket). The answer is a bool (GetBool). In DEVICE the kind name (weapon/armor1/armor2/abChip, strings 519138..519150) is skipped for ISMAX and an unknown name or an operation beyond ISEQUIP pushes no answer.

**Notes:** Decoded from src/lua/api.c wrapper bodies, rodata strings 50d9d8/50d9e8/50d9d0 and the 3fca10 disassembly; native handlers are dw4_playertransactions_folder_call (port/src/inventorydetail.c) chained after dw4_playerrecords_call at the hub, engine and test bindings.

**Evidence:** port/docs/prmame-npc.md

<a id="f958"></a>
### #958 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004482a0 — beNDMWItemWeapon/BlueArmor/YellowArmor/AbilityChip/Disk vfunc_23/24, FUN_003ef1e0

Item id word (3ef1e0 decode, beNDMWItem* vfunc_23 encode / vfunc_24 decode): bits 0-9 record, bit 10 equipped, bits 12-15 kind. Kinds 0 weapon and 1 blue armor add pocket (bits 16-19) and revision (bits 20-30); kind 2 yellow armor adds revision (bits 20-30); kind 3 ability chip adds nothing; kind 4 disk puts its quantity in bits 16-19. A kind nibble above 4, including 0xFFFFFFFF, is an empty entry (3ef1e0 stores NULL).

**Notes:** Read from the five class methods (4482a0.., 448360, 4483b0, 448500, 4485d0, 448680, 448730, 448770) and the 3ef1e0 switch; the codec reproduces every item word of six retail-written saves byte for byte (port/tools/test_saveimage.c).

<a id="f959"></a>
### #959 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003ee6d0 — FUN_003ee6d0 / FUN_003ef5d0 / dw4_saveimage_*

Save file = two mirrored 0xA000 blocks holding FOUR character records, one per player slot, 0x2000 apart from file offset 8. Deliver-struct offsets from serializer 3ee6d0 / loader 3ef5d0: ISUSE 0, UNIQUE 4, DIGIMONNAME 8, name marker 0x28 (0xFFFF) + u16 codes 0x2A, LEVEL/HP/MHP/MP/MMP 0x48..0x58, XDATA 0x5C, BIT 0x60, DEVICEFOLDER 0x64 (36), DISKFOLDER 0xF4 (12), CARDLIST 0x124 (50 bytes), BANKDEVICE 0x158 (96), BANKBIT 0x2D8, equipment indices 0x2DC/0x2E8/0x2FC/0x300/0x314, BASE_FLAG 0x318, BASE_FLAGFOLDER 0x718, BASE_COUNTER 0x724, BASE_ISUSE 0x764, BASE_LEVEL 0x774, BASE_EXP 0x7B4, BASE_SKILL 0x7F4, BASE_UPCNT 0xA34 (176 words, ends 0xCF4). Equipment lists store the DEVICEFOLDER position of the equipped object.

**Notes:** Six retail-written files (fresh, two level-up saves, a level-999 save, a two- and a four-character resave) load into records and store back byte-identical; slot stride seen at 0x2008/0x4008/0x6008 and SaveCtrl loops four slots. Extends #44/#141 (single-record view).

**Evidence:** port/docs/save-image.md

<a id="f964"></a>
### #964 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003723c0 — SYSctlFlagFolder wrapper 3723c0 -> beNDMWGameRam CTRLITEM FLAG branch

CONFIRMED SYSctlFlagFolder(op,index) is the GAMERAM CTRLITEM receiver (3fca10) with group FLAG over the SESSION FLAGFOLDER (12 bytes; not the per-player BASE_FLAGFOLDER): SET stores 1 and answers true only from 0, CLR stores 0 and answers true only from set, IS answers the byte unchanged (retail pushes the raw u8). Writes are bounds-checked, reads are not; native refuses an out-of-range index.

<a id="f965"></a>
### #965 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00407120 — beNDMWSaveCtrl vfunc_31/34

beNDMWSaveCtrl update (407120, 2368 B) is the scene controller's driver, now implemented as src/savectrl.c: phase 1 asks SAVEUTILITY (3f1270/3f12f0) for card readiness; phase 2 opens j_chrselback (SQ_OPEN), generates SAVEINTF_ALL, waits for COMMONINTF +0x30 == 3, forces the four player items closed (+0x24=2) and waits for all +0xE == 1, fades with i_fadewn01 SQ_BLOUT30, closes j_chrselback and sends SETSELECT_DATA with GAMERAM GETCOMMON (BKCHIPSTAGE entry cleared for each player whose +0xD != 1, NINZU = the count of +0xD == 1); phase 3 releases the card (3f1380/3f13d0) and leaves: no player with +0xD == 1 -> STAGECTL NEXTSTAGE stagis10, otherwise PLAYERPOINT SETGENERATEPOS 2 and NEXTSTAGE stagML12. The exit counts players that chose data (+0xD), not used card slots (earlier wording corrected). vfunc_34 (407aa0) answers SAVECTRL GENERATE by building the state once. No case 4: the controller idles after the exit request.

**Notes:** Native sequencing implemented from the m2c draft plus decoded strings (517fd0, 5194c0..519608); tools/test_savectrl.c asserts the retail order of every send, every wait state and both exits. Not an instruction-level oracle (needs the messenger/property objects). The exit contradiction with pack 810's chunk_0000 is resolved in #967.

**Evidence:** port/docs/save-keeper.md; port/src/savectrl.c; port/tools/test_savectrl.c; scratch m2c drafts of 407120/407aa0

<a id="f966"></a>
### #966 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x004081a0 — beNDMWSaveIntf vfunc_31/34

beNDMWSaveIntf update (4081a0, 16,352 B) is a per-player window state machine (player in +8, state +0x24 in 0..9, sub-state +0x28/+0x2C/+0x30) over the receivers CTRL, COMMDLCTRL, MESWIN, SELSLOT, COMMONINTF, DEGISTATECTRL and DATASELINTF; it opens j_chrselbase and j_chrselmesswin and sends text call numbers (player<<16)|0x51C/0x502/0x1004..0x3024 handled by the shared TITLEFONT01/CHRSELFONT chunk (entry_0810 chunk_0005, calls 10..216). It also issues GAMERAM GETPLAYER, CURRENT2BASE and GAMESTART, so it is a shared data-select interface rather than a save-only screen.

**Notes:** Draft only (m2c) and unconfirmed. Item state machine: 0 runs the CTRL control machine (0..9) over j_chrselbase/j_chrselmesswin/SELSLOT/DATASELINTF; control state 8 sets the player's +0xD (data chosen) and waits for every participating player; item state 2 closes the windows and sets +0xE; 3/4/6 handle card status codes 2..12 with per-mode text sets 0x1xxx/0x2xxx/0x3xxx; 7 forces all items to 8; 8 sets +0xF. State 0 takes the ISUSE branch (CURRENT2BASE) for players with a character and GAMESTART (player 0) otherwise, so one pack serves both the Save Keeper and a fresh boot. About 40 KB / 91 helper functions (3d9de0..3f13d0) create the windows, read the slot list, perform the card requests (3f1490) and format notices; none recovered.

**Evidence:** port/docs/save-keeper.md

The stagIS12 pack's chunk_0000 (SETGENERATEPOS 3 / NEXTSTAGE stagML11) is gatedisc.lua, the shared disc-gate template, not the save screen's exit: pack 810's seven named Lua chunks in byte order are gatedisc (0), first (1), boot (2), func (3), start (4), txt_mem (5), txt_Tar (6); the executable names gatedisc.lua in exactly one function, beNDMWItemDisk vfunc_22 (4487a0, load at 448a6c); neither stagIS12 pack (NDMW entries 809, 810) contains an ItemDisk object. The scene is left by beNDMWSaveCtrl's native exit (#965): Central at generate position 2 when a player chose data, the title otherwise.

The conclusion that the scene exits through SaveCtrl is inferred from that and is not observed in a retail run; a retail trace of the hub save flow (arrival after the save) would confirm it.

**Where:** 0x003c4870 — beNDMWLoadCtrl2 / beNDMWLoadIntf2 register/vfunc table

The stagIS13 load screen runs on the beNDMWLoadCtrl2 / beNDMWLoadIntf2 families (NameSel, ChrDataSel, SelSlot, Diffselect, DataSelIntf, DegiState*, MakeChr, MesWin, ComMdlCtrl, TextOrder, SlotPlayer*, SavePlWork*): 13 update bodies of vfunc_21 and up totalling 31,704 bytes, distinct classes from SaveIntf, so the recovered SaveIntf helpers do not cover it.

**Notes:** Counted from config/symbols.txt (Gap__Bec__beNDMWLoad*__vfunc_21+). Recovery is open. REFUTED in part by the IS13 recovery: LoadIntf2 (3e4750) calls the same 85-helper toolkit as SaveIntf (3d9de0..3f1460, e.g. the panel, selector, slot-chooser, message-window and card helpers); only the NameSel/Diffselect/MakeChr windows and the update bodies are new. See finding 972. Folded into #972, the current answer for stagIS13 load screen runs on LoadIntf2/LoadCtrl2; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** port/docs/save-keeper.md; config/symbols.txt

**Where:** 0x003f1a80 — beNDMWLoadCtrl2 vfunc_31/vfunc_34

beNDMWLoadCtrl2 update (3f1a80, 2,512 bytes) and its GENERATE handler (3f2490) are SaveCtrl's state machine driving LOADINTF2_ALL: card scan, j_chrselback open, interface scene state 0/2/3, player close, i_fadewn01 SQ_BLOUT30, SETSELECT_DATA, GETCOMMON NINZU, card release. The hub-mode exit (GENERATE without integers) is PLAYERPOINT SETGENERATEPOS 4 then STAGECTL NEXTSTAGE stagML11 whatever the player count; the integer-carrying GENERATE is the title mode (SQ_WTIN60 fade wait, TITLE2 GAMESTART with DIFFICULTY/CAMERAMODE/LIGHTMODE, gameStart.lua, position 0).

**Notes:** Strings decoded from the ELF (518130..518280, 5182a0..5182d0); native in src/savectrl.c (dw4_loadctrl_generate/exit_stage). Sequencing, not an instruction oracle. Title mode is not ported.

### #973 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0040da00 — Terminal Digi-Elf stagIS13 flow

stagIS13 in the single-player hub: the Terminal n_digilff[0] reaches it only when SYSgetPlayerNinzu ~= 4 (chunk_0033). Player 0 already has a character, so LoadIntf2 shows the panel and notice 0x521 'To log into the Digital World press the START button', START finishes the scene, and LoadCtrl2 publishes NINZU=1 and returns to stagML11 at generate position 4 without writing a save. It is the join/Continue screen for additional players (GAMEDATAINIT for ISUSE~=1 players 1..3), not a slot loader for player 0.

**Notes:** check-hubload drives the real NPC; rendered frame inspected (four player panels, status window, START notice). The retail arrival at position 4 is derived statically.

<a id="f991"></a>
### #991 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0058a1c0 — beMessenger -> beMessengerArgData transform

Static chain to the focused model's world position: p = *(*(*(0x58a1c0)+0x10)+0xc0)+0x30 (x,y,z,1.0). The middle object is a beMessengerArgData, so the position is that of the model the engine is currently processing. Live-tested: it follows the player under free control in Central (6/6 stick pushes) and the Terminal (4/4 from a different save lineage, op-ready; 2/4 on the gate pad where the other directions are blocked). In a scripted stage with no message context (practice tutorial stag0001) the object is null.

**Notes:** Verified live with stick pushes; not a registry lookup, so only the hubs are covered. A registry path to the player was not found at depth <= 4.

**Evidence:** live PCSX2 experiment and savestate analysis; driver tool kept outside the repository (../extras/dw4-tools/emu_state.py)

<a id="f992"></a>
### #992 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0058a1c0 — beModelCtrlNode2 position

The player's authoritative world position is the scene node (a beModelCtrlNode2) translation row at node+0x50. Five copies of the vector exist (AI data +0x70 in a beModelCtrlAIData, a beBaseInfoRamList record, the message-argument matrix, the node, a beCopyModelCtrl2); writing to the node propagates to all of them and moves the character on screen while it keeps responding to the stick; writing to the AI data, message matrix or render copy is reverted by the engine and the base-info write stays isolated.

**Notes:** Live write experiment per copy in the Terminal (op-ready), plus a Central teleport of 190 units with a screen capture.

**Evidence:** live PCSX2 experiment and savestate analysis; driver tool kept outside the repository (../extras/dw4-tools/emu_state.py)

<a id="f993"></a>
### #993 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0056df40 — player model owner

The player's model owner is o = *(*(0x56df40)+0x84); the scene node is *(o+0xe0) and the player info object is *(o+0xe4). This path does not depend on the message-argument object, which is only populated intermittently. Valid in five savestates from different lineages and live; not available in the Terminal gate-menu state.

**Notes:** Reverse pointer search from the node (7,438 chains) filtered by agreement with the known node in four other states.

**Evidence:** live PCSX2 experiment and savestate analysis; driver tool kept outside the repository (../extras/dw4-tools/emu_state.py)

<a id="f994"></a>
### #994 · confirmed

**Where:** beNDMWMdlPlayerInfoWork yaw

The player's facing is stored in a beNDMWMdlPlayerInfoWork: yaw in radians at +0x84 (0 faces +z, positive towards +x) and the last applied yaw (normalised to 0..2pi) at +0x9c. The engine re-derives the node's rotation rows (right = (cos,0,-sin), forward = (sin,0,cos), 4x4 row-major from node+0x20) when +0x84 differs from +0x9c and then updates +0x9c; writing both with equal values is ignored, and writing only the node rows is unreliable.

**Notes:** Found by searching RAM for the node heading in radians; verified with six write variants and headings 90, 180, -90, 45, 0, -120 held for 1.5 s with normal stick control afterwards.

**Evidence:** live PCSX2 experiment and savestate analysis; driver tool kept outside the repository (../extras/dw4-tools/emu_state.py)

<a id="f996"></a>
### #996 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0010bb60 — sync-after-call wrappers

A call wrapper whose retail body ends with a memory barrier is `{ callee(args); asm { sync } }` in MWCC C++ mode: the barrier sits after the call's delay slot and before the frame restore. Six such wrappers (0x10bb60, 0x10bb80, 0x10bba0, 0x10bbc0, 0x4831a0 with `a0 & 0xffff` in the delay slot, and 0x10c008 with a trailing `ei` and a zero return) now match byte for byte.

**Notes:** Inline `asm { sync }` and `asm { ei }` compile to the exact retail words; the frame is kept only in C++ mode (finding #314).

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/FUN_0010bb60.cpp.. FUN_0010c008.cpp; tools/verify.py MATCH

<a id="f997"></a>
### #997 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004482a0 — beNDMWItem*::vfunc_21 data-base request

The eight beNDMWItem* classes' `vfunc_21` (0x4482a0, 0x4483f0, 0x448540, 0x448640, 0x4486f0, 0x448ab0, 0x448ee0, 0x448f10) forward their two arguments to beNDMWItemBase_RequestDataBase (0x447bc0) with the class's own static data-base table (e.g. DAT_0051d580 for the weapon item) as the third argument. Seven match as C++ pass-through wrappers (the eighth, AbilityChip, was already matched inside beNDMWItemBaseDatabase.c).

**Notes:** The generic wargs template did not bank these; int pass-through parameters plus a `char *` table address reproduce the retail words exactly.

**Evidence:** src/unmatched/Gap__Bec__beNDMWItem*__vfunc_21.cpp; tools/verify.py MATCH

<a id="f998"></a>
### #998 · confirmed

**Where:** automatch candidate selection

tools/automatch.py chose its candidates by checking only the output directory for an existing file, so it re-banked about 2,300 functions that were already defined inside folded multi-function translation units and `make check` then failed with duplicate definitions. It now skips every name tools/units.scan reports as defined anywhere in src/. With the fix its real candidate set is 97 functions and the generic templates match none, so the remaining unmatched small functions need shape-specific work.

**Notes:** Reproduced: a run wrote 2,266 files and `make check` raised `src/lua/api.c defines BLTgetShootRamNo, already defined in src/lua/api.c`; removed and fixed.

**Evidence:** tools/automatch.py; tools/units.py scan

<a id="f999"></a>
### #999 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003e4750 — beNDMWLoadIntf2 update (vfunc_31) control states 6..8, item states 5/6

The title's Call Digimon screens are beNDMWLoadIntf2 control states 3 (Create/Call) -> 6 (memory-card chooser, SelSlot) -> item state 5 (card check, FILEFIND, READ_MEDIA behind the "Accessing memory card" notice) -> 7 (DATASELINTF in load mode: the 2x2 data areas with a status panel for the highlighted one, "Select the Digimon to call.") -> 8 (Select Difficulty when hostLoad.lua allows it, then the log-in prompt and START). A live retail run showed exactly this order, text and cancel behaviour (Circle: prompt -> chooser, grid -> chooser, chooser -> Create/Call).

The Create/Call window (state 3) stays in the character UI; the title scene starts at state 6 and reports cancel when state 3 is reached again.

**Evidence:** port/src/saveintf.c; port/tools/test_titlecall.c; recovered/frontend/loadintf2.c

<a id="f1000"></a>
### #1000 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003ddb80 — beNDMWLoadIntf2Diffselect 3ddb10 / 3ddb80

beNDMWLoadIntf2Diffselect: 3ddb10 initialises it (cursor 0, rows 2 for kind 0 = j_linewin02, 3 for kind 1 = j_linewin03, player, flags clear) and 3ddb80 runs it: open the window (SQ_OPEN, text 0x402 or 0x403, notice 0x404), move with SQ_LINEUP00/01 and SQ_LINEDW01/02 clamped to the rows, Cross stores the row (+0x1C), Circle sets +0x25, then the closing line sequence (SQ_LINEON00/01/02 or SQ_CANCEL) and +0x24 = 1.
